\langle\bar{G}\odot(ss^{\top})y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{16k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{640\rho_{\gamma}kn_{i}}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,,

where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

Majority voting for pairwise communities

We give a similar theorem for bounding the error of majority voting for each pair of communities.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
C_{d,\varepsilon}=(\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b})^{2}
.
Let
\gamma\in[0,1-\frac{1000\chi k}{\varepsilon\sqrt{d}}]
.
Choose
\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\exp(-\gamma C_{d,\varepsilon}/k)
.
Then with probability at least
1-\exp(-100k)-\frac{1}{n^{3}}
, for every
z\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
, and for every pair of communities
y\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,\pm 1}}\right\}^{n}
.
We have

\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{8k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{96\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,,

where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

Similarly, for establishing the feasibility of the program constraints in
Section

11
, We
need the following corollary, which bounds the error of majority voting when a small fraction of the vertices are removed from the graph.

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
\gamma\in[0,1-\frac{1000\chi k}{\varepsilon\sqrt{d}}]
.
Choose
\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\exp(-\gamma C)
.
Then with probability at least
1-\exp(-100k)-\frac{2}{n^{3}}
, for every
z\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
, for every
s\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
such that
\lVert s\rVert_{1}\geqslant(1-\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon}))n
, and for every pair of communities
y\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,\pm 1}}\right\}^{n}
.
We have

\langle\bar{G}\odot(ss^{\top})y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{16k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{640\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,,

where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

5
Algorithmic framework and results

In this section, we give a sketch of our robust optimal recovery algorithm (see
Algorithm

11.1
) with its key building blocks.

Algorithm

11.1
contains two main steps:
initialization
and
boosting based on pairwise majority voting
.
The goal of initialization is to get a
1/\poly(k)
approximation such that, in each community, we have error rate at most
0.001
.
Given the
1/\poly(k)
-initial estimation, we can apply pairwise majority voting to boost the accuracy to the minimax rate.

5.1
Robust bisection algorithm

The key component of our initialization algorithm is the following robust bisectioning algorithm that finds bisections in the graph with bisection error
\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\bigl(1-o(1)\bigr)\,\frac{C_{d,\varepsilon}}{k^{2}}}}\right)+\poly(k)\eta
.

Algorithm 5.1

(Robust bisection algorithm)
.

Input:
A graph
G
sampled from the
k
-stochastic block model
\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
with
\eta n
corrupted nodes.

1.

Graph splitting:
We let
G_{1}
be the graph obtained by subsampling each edge in
G
independently with probability
0.99
and let
G_{2}\coloneqq G\setminus G_{1}
.

2.

Rough initialization:
Run a rough
k
-clustering algorithm on graph
G_{1}
to obtain rough initialization
Z_{\text{rough}}
with error rate
0.001+10^{4}\eta
.

3.

Identifying well recovered blocks:
Use graph
G_{2}
and
Z_{\text{rough}}
to identify
k/2
clusters in which
0.99
-fraction of the nodes belongs to the same community. Construct rough bisection
x_{\text{rough}}
by putting the
k/2
identified clusters on one side of the bisection.

4.

Bisection boosting:
Use majority voting on bisections with
x_{\text{rough}}
as the initialization to obtain a bisection
\hat{x}
with error rate
\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\bigl(1-o(1)\bigr)\,\frac{C_{d,\varepsilon}}{k^{2}}}}\right)+\poly(k)\eta
.

Output:

\hat{x}
.

We will now briefly explain and state the results for the subroutines
rough initialization
,
identifying well recovered blocks
and
bisection boosting
, then state the initialization guarantee that is achieved by recursively applying the bisection algorithm.

Rough initialization

Previous work based on semidefinite programming and spectral algorithms can get a rough initialization with error
0.001+10^{4}\,k\,\eta
(Lemma 7.3 of
[
38
]
). In our work, we show that a rough initialization procedure based on Sum-of-Squares (SoS) can get an improved guarantee (see
Section

6
for details).

Theorem 5.2

(Robust rough initialization)
.

Let
(G^{\circ},Z^{\circ})\sim\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
be generated from the
k
-stochastic block model and
G
be generated by adversarially corrupting
\eta
-fraction of the nodes in
G^{\circ}
.
Assume
k\leqslant n^{0.001}
,
\eta\leqslant\frac{1}{\poly(k)}
,
d=o(n)
, and
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2}
for some sufficiently large constant
K
.
There exists a polynomial-time algorithm that, given observation of
G
, outputs an estimator
\hat{Z}\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
such that, with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-\tfrac{1}{\poly(n)}
,

\text{error}_{k}(\hat{Z},Z^{\circ})\leqslant 0.001+10^{4}\,\eta.

Identifying well recovered clusters

Once we have a rough initialization with misclassification rate at most
0.001
, at least
k/2
clusters must be “well recovered”, in the sense that at least
0.99
of the nodes in these well recovered clusters belong to the same community.
We show that there exists an algorithm that can robustly identify these well recovered clusters (see
Section

7
for details).

Theorem 5.3

(Robust identification of well recovered clusters)
.

Let
(G^{\circ},Z^{\circ})\sim\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
be generated from the
k
-stochastic block model and
G
be generated by adversarially corrupting
\eta
-fraction of the nodes in
G^{\circ}
.
Assume
k\leqslant n^{0.001}
,
\eta\leqslant\frac{1}{\poly(k)}
,
d=o(n)
, and
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2}
for some sufficiently large constant
K
.
For any set
S\subset V
of
n/k
vertices, there exists a polynomial-time algorithm that, with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-\tfrac{1}{\poly(n)}
, outputs

•

YES if at least
0.99
of the vertices in
S
belong to the same community;

•

NO if no more than
0.98
of the vertices in
S
belong to any single community.

Bisection boosting

Given
k/2
well recovered clusters, we can construct a rough bisection
x_{\text{rough}}
by putting them on one side of the bisection.
We show that, given this rough bisection with error rate
0.001
, we can use majority voting to boost the bisection accuracy to obtain a bisection with optimal bisection error (see
Section

8
for details).

Theorem 5.4

(Robust bisection boosting)
.

Let
(G^{\circ},Z^{\circ})\sim\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
be generated from the
k
-stochastic block model and
G
be generated by adversarially corrupting
\eta
-fraction of the nodes in
G^{\circ}
.
Assume
k\leqslant n^{0.001}
,
\eta\leqslant\frac{1}{\poly(k)}
,
d=o(n)
, and
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2}
for some sufficiently large constant
K
.
Let
S_{1},S_{2},\ldots,S_{k/2}\subset V
be disjoint subsets of size
n/k
such that in every
S_{i}
at least
0.99
-fraction of the nodes belong to the same community.
Let
x^{\circ}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
be the ground-truth community bisection with the underlying communities of
S_{1},S_{2},\ldots,S_{k/2}
on the same side.
There exists a polynomial-time algorithm that, given observation of
G
and
S_{1},S_{2},\ldots,S_{k/2}
, outputs
\hat{x}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
such that, with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-\tfrac{1}{\poly(n)}
,

\frac{1}{n}\|\hat{x}-x^{\circ}\|^{2}\leqslant\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\bigl(1-o(1)\bigr)\frac{\tilde{C}}{8}}}\right)+\poly(k)\eta\,,

where
\tilde{C}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{a^{\frac{2}{k}}b^{1-\frac{2}{k}}}-\sqrt{b}}}\right)^{2}
is the bisection SNR.

Robust bisection algorithm

As a corollary, by combining
Theorem

5.2
,
Theorem

5.3
and
Theorem

5.4
, we have a robust bisection algorithm for the
k
-stochastic block model with optimal bisection error rate (see
Section

9
for details).

Theorem 5.5

(Robust bisection algorithm)
.

Let
(G^{\circ},Z^{\circ})\sim\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
be generated from the
k
-stochastic block model and
G
be generated by adversarially corrupting
\eta
-fraction of the nodes in
G^{\circ}
.
Assume
k\leqslant n^{0.001}
,
\eta\leqslant\frac{1}{\poly(k)}
,
d=o(n)
, and
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2}
for some sufficiently large constant
K
.
There exists a polynomial-time algorithm that, given observation of
G
, outputs
\hat{x}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
such that, with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-\tfrac{1}{\poly(n)}
,

\frac{1}{n}\|\hat{x}-x^{\circ}\|^{2}\,\leqslant\,\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\bigl(1-o(1)\bigr)\frac{\tilde{C}}{8}}}\right)+O(k\eta).

where
\tilde{C}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{a^{\frac{2}{k}}b^{1-\frac{2}{k}}}-\sqrt{b}}}\right)^{2}
is the bisection SNR and
x^{\circ}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
is a true community bisection of
G^{\circ}
.

5.2
Robust initialization for symmetry breaking

By recursively applying
Theorem

5.5
, we can cluster the vertices into
k
communities with error rate
\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\bigl(1-o(1)\bigr)\,\frac{C_{d,\varepsilon}}{k^{2}}}}\right)+\poly(k)\eta
(see
Section

10
for details).
When
C_{d,\varepsilon}\geqslant\Omega(k^{2}\log k)
, we get an error rate of
1/\poly(k)+\poly(k)\eta
.

Theorem 5.6

(Initialization for symmetry breaking)
.

Let
(G^{\circ},Z^{\circ})\sim\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
be generated from the
k
-stochastic block model and
G
be generated by adversarially corrupting
\eta
-fraction of the nodes in
G^{\circ}
.
Assume
k\leqslant n^{0.001}
,
\eta\leqslant\frac{1}{\poly(k)}
,
d=o(n)
, and
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2}
for some sufficiently large constant
K
.
There exists a polynomial-time algorithm that, given observation of
G
, outputs an estimator
\hat{Z}\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
such that, with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-\tfrac{1}{\poly(n)}
,

\text{error}_{k}(\hat{Z},Z^{\circ})\,\leqslant\,\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\bigl(1-o(1)\bigr)\,\frac{C_{d,\varepsilon}}{k^{2}}}}\right)+\poly(k)\eta.

5.3
Boosting via pairwise majority voting

Given the initialization with error
1/\poly(k)+\poly(k)\eta
, we can apply pairwise majority voting to boost the accuracy of the estimator to the minimax rate.
The formal main theorem of our robust clustering algorithm for node-corrupted k-stochastic block model is as follows.
The algorithm and proofs of
Theorem

5.7
are presented in
Section

11
.

Theorem 5.7

(Robust recovery in k-SBM with optimal rate)
.

Let
(G^{\circ},Z^{\circ})\sim\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
be generated from the
k
-stochastic block model and
G
be generated by adversarially corrupting
\eta
-fraction of the nodes in
G^{\circ}
.
Assume
k\leqslant n^{0.001}
,
\eta\leqslant\frac{1}{\poly(k)}
,
d=o(n)
, and
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2}\log k
for some sufficiently large constant
K
.
There exists a polynomial-time algorithm (see
Algorithm

11.1
) that, given observation of
G
, outputs an estimator
\hat{Z}\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
such that, with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-\tfrac{1}{\poly(n)}
,

\text{error}_{k}(\hat{Z},Z^{\circ})\leqslant\exp\Bigl(-\bigl(1-o(1)\bigr)\frac{C_{d,\varepsilon}}{k}\Bigr)+\poly(k)\cdot\eta\,.

6
Robust rough initialization

In this section, we prove
Theorem

5.2
via a natural SoS-based initialization procedure.
Our analysis is of independent interest as it both simplifies and strictly improves upon the initialization guarantees of
[
38
]
.
In particular, we distinguish two regimes based on the adversarial corruption fraction
\eta
:

•

If
\eta\sim\exp\bigl(-C_{d,\varepsilon}/k\bigr)
(the critical, minimax regime),
our goal is to obtain an initial recovery error bounded by a small constant (e.g.
0.001
).

•

If
\eta
is significantly larger than
\exp\bigl(-C_{d,\varepsilon}/k\bigr)
,
the recovery error is dominated by the corruption level,
meaning that one can only hope to recover the community structure up to the inherent corruption threshold.

In the former case, both our method and that of
[
38
]
achieve the desired
0.001
recovery error,
which is sufficient to bootstrap our subsequent boosting step.
In the latter regime, however, our SoS formulation offers a significant improvement by tolerating a constant corruption fraction
\eta
independently of
k
.
By contrast, the guarantee in
[
38
]
requires that
\eta\lesssim 1/k
in order to maintain the misclassification error
(on the order of
k\eta
) bounded, rendering their bound vacuous as
k
diverges.

Theorem

(Restatement of
Theorem

5.2
)
.

Let
(G^{\circ},Z^{\circ})\sim\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
be generated from the
k
-stochastic block model and
G
be generated by adversarially corrupting
\eta
-fraction of the nodes in
G^{\circ}
.
Assume
k\leqslant n^{0.001}
,
\eta\leqslant\frac{1}{\poly(k)}
,
d=o(n)
, and
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2}
for some sufficiently large constant
K
.
There exists a polynomial-time algorithm that, given observation of
G
, outputs an estimator
\hat{Z}\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
such that, with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-\tfrac{1}{\poly(n)}
,

\text{error}_{k}(\hat{Z},Z^{\circ})\leqslant 0.001+10^{4}\,\eta.

We consider a constant-degree sum-of-squares relaxation for the following program:

\mathcal{A}_{\text{init}}(\xi,G,Z;G_{\text{input}},\eta)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} &\xi\odot\xi=\xi,\quad\langle\xi,\Ind\rangle\geqslant\Bigl(1-\eta-\exp\Bigl(-\frac{2\varepsilon^{2}d}{k}\Bigr)\Bigr)n,\\[4.30554pt]
&Z\odot Z=Z,\quad\sum_{a\in[k]}Z(i,a)=1,\quad\sum_{i\in[n]}Z(i,a)=\frac{n}{k},\\[4.30554pt]
&\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\,G-\frac{d}{n}\,11^{\top}-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}\,(ZZ^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}\,J)\,}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d},\\[4.30554pt]
&G\odot\xi\xi^{\top}=G_{\text{input}}\odot\xi\xi^{\top}.\end{aligned}}}\right\}

(6.1)

For simplicity of notation, we let

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{bool}}(\xi,Z)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\xi\odot\xi=\xi,Z\odot Z=Z,\sum_{a\in[k]}Z(i,a)=1,\sum_{i\in[n]}Z(i,a)=n/k}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{spec}}(G,Z)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\,G-\frac{d}{n}\,11^{\top}-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}\,(ZZ^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}\,J)\,}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{corr}}(\xi,G;G_{\text{input}},\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{G\odot\xi\xi^{\top}=G_{\text{input}}\odot\xi\xi^{\top},\langle\xi,\Ind\rangle\geqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\eta-\exp(-2\varepsilon^{2}d/k)}}\right)n}}\right\}

Then we have

\mathcal{A}_{\text{init}}(\xi,G,Z;G_{\text{input}},\eta)=\mathcal{A}_{\text{bool}}(\xi,Z)\cup\mathcal{A}_{\text{spec}}(G,Z)\cup\mathcal{A}_{\text{corr}}(\xi,G;G_{\text{input}},\eta)\,.

We first show that the program is feasible with high probability.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
G_{\mathrm{input}}
be an
\eta
-corrupted stochastic block model (SBM) graph on
n
nodes partitioned into
k
communities. Suppose that

\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant K\,k^{2},

for a sufficiently large constant
K>0
.
Then, with high probability, the feasibility conditions in
Eq.

6.1

are satisfied by the choice
\xi=\xi_{0}\odot\mathbf{1}_{S}
(where
S
is as in Cor.
B.2
),

Z=Z^{\circ}
, and a suitable
G
agreeing with
G_{\mathrm{input}}
on the subgraph induced by the set of vertices indicated by
\xi
.

Proof.

By Corollary
B.2
, there exists a subset
S\subseteq[n]
with

|S|\geqslant\bigl(1-\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon})\bigr)n
such that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\Bigl(G^{\circ}-\frac{d}{n}J-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}\,\bigl({Z^{\circ}}{Z^{\circ}}^{\top}-\tfrac{1}{k}J\bigr)\Bigr)\odot\mathbf{1}_{S}\mathbf{1}_{S}^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d}\kern 5.0pt,

where
\chi
is a universal constant.
Let
\xi
be the indicator of the uncorrupted nodes intersected with
S
, i.e.,

\xi:=\xi_{0}\odot\mathbf{1}_{S}
, and take
Z^{\circ}\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
to be the ground-truth membership matrix.
Define
G
to agree with
G_{\mathrm{input}}
on the block indexed by
\supp(\xi)
and to equal the model mean

\frac{d}{n}J+\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}\bigl(Z^{\circ}(Z^{\circ})^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}J\bigr)
outside this block.
Then the centered matrix

G-\frac{d}{n}J-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}\bigl(Z^{\circ}(Z^{\circ})^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}J\bigr)
vanishes outside
\supp(\xi)
and,
on
\supp(\xi)
, its operator norm is bounded by
\chi\sqrt{d}
by
Corollary
B.2
.
Hence the spectral constraint in

Eq.

6.1
holds.
Moreover,

\langle\xi,\Ind\rangle\geqslant\bigl(1-\eta-\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon})\bigr)n
, which satisfies

\mathcal{A}_{\text{corr}}
. Therefore, with these choices of
\xi
,
Z=Z^{\circ}
, and
G
, all feasibility
conditions in
Eq.

6.1
are satisfied with high probability.
∎

Now, we give the SoS proof for the recovery guarantees.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
G_{\text{input}}
be an
\eta
-corrupted SBM graph with
n
nodes and
k
communities, and assume that

\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2},

for some sufficiently large constant
K
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{init}}(\xi^{(1)},G^{(1)},Z^{(1)};G_{\text{input}},\eta),\quad\mathcal{A}_{\text{init}}(\xi^{(2)},G^{(2)},Z^{(2)};G_{\text{input}},\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{111.17741pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-111.17741pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[111.17741pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{8}$}}}\hskip-111.17741pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[111.17741pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\xi^{(1)},\,\xi^{(2)},\,G^{(1)},\,G^{(2)},\,Z^{(1)},\,Z^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert Z^{(1)}(Z^{(1)})^{\top}-Z^{(2)}(Z^{(2)})^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leqslant(0.001+10\eta)n^{2}/k

\displaystyle\,.

Proof.

Since the constraints
G^{(1)}\odot\xi^{(1)}(\xi^{(1)})^{\top}=G_{\text{input}}\odot\xi^{(1)}(\xi^{(1)})^{\top}
and
G^{(2)}\odot\xi^{(2)}(\xi^{(2)})^{\top}=G_{\text{input}}\odot\xi^{(2)}(\xi^{(2)})^{\top}
hold, let
\xi=\xi^{(1)}\odot\xi^{(2)}
. We then have a degree-
8
sum-of-squares proof that

G^{(1)}\odot\xi\xi^{\top}=G_{\text{input}}\odot\xi\xi^{\top}\quad\text{and}\quad G^{(2)}\odot\xi\xi^{\top}=G_{\text{input}}\odot\xi\xi^{\top}\,.

Therefore, we have
G^{(1)}\odot\xi\xi^{\top}=G^{(2)}\odot\xi\xi^{\top}
.
By the min–max characterization of the spectral norm,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{bool}}(\xi^{(1)},Z^{(1)})\cup\mathcal{A}_{\text{spec}}(G^{(1)},Z^{(1)})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{37.06781pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-37.06781pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[37.06781pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{8}$}}}\hskip-37.06781pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[37.06781pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{G^{(1)},Z^{(1)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G^{(1)}-\frac{d}{n}J-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}(Z^{(1)}(Z^{(1)})^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}\,J)\,}}\right)\odot\xi\xi^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{bool}}(\xi^{(2)},Z^{(2)})\cup\mathcal{A}_{\text{spec}}(G^{(2)},Z^{(2)})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{37.06781pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-37.06781pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[37.06781pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{8}$}}}\hskip-37.06781pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[37.06781pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{G^{(2)},Z^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G^{(2)}-\frac{d}{n}J-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}(Z^{(2)}(Z^{(2)})^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}\,J)\,}}\right)\odot\xi\xi^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d}

By the triangle inequality, this yields a degree-8 sum-of-squares certificate that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Z^{(1)}(Z^{(1)})^{\top}-Z^{(2)}(Z^{(2)})^{\top}}}\right)\odot\xi\xi^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant 2\chi\sqrt{d}\,.

Since
Z^{(1)}
and
Z^{(2)}
are community membership matrices, each Gram matrix

Z^{(t)}(Z^{(t)})^{\top}
has rank at most
k
, so

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Z^{(1)}(Z^{(1)})^{\top}-Z^{(2)}(Z^{(2)})^{\top}}}\right)\odot\xi\xi^{\top}
has rank at most
2k
12
12

12

Indeed, letting
D=\mathrm{diag}(\xi)
and

\Delta=Z^{(1)}(Z^{(1)})^{\top}-Z^{(2)}(Z^{(2)})^{\top}
, we have

\Delta\odot\xi\xi^{\top}=D\Delta D
, hence

\mathrm{rank}(\Delta\odot\xi\xi^{\top})\leqslant\mathrm{rank}(\Delta)\leqslant 2k
.
.
Thus, we obtain a degree-
8
sum-of-squares proof that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Z^{(1)}(Z^{(1)})^{\top}-Z^{(2)}(Z^{(2)})^{\top}}}\right)\odot\xi\xi^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leqslant\frac{8\chi^{2}kn^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}d}\,.

Finally, since
\lVert\xi\rVert_{1}\geqslant(1-2\eta-2\exp(-2\varepsilon^{2}d/k))n
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{init}}(\xi^{(1)},G^{(1)},Z^{(1)};G_{\text{input}},\eta),\mathcal{A}_{\text{init}}(\xi^{(2)},G^{(2)},Z^{(2)};G_{\text{input}},\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{102.84428pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-102.84428pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[102.84428pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{8}$}}}\hskip-102.84428pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[102.84428pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)},G^{(1)},G^{(2)},Z^{(1)},Z^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert Z^{(1)}(Z^{(1)})^{\top}-Z^{(2)}(Z^{(2)})^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leqslant\frac{8\chi^{2}kn^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}d}+10\eta n^{2}/k\,.

∎

Finally, we conclude with the rounding step.

Proof of
Theorem

5.2
.

Let us write
M^{\circ}\coloneqq Z^{\circ}(Z^{\circ})^{\top}
for the ground-truth matrix.
By
Lemma

6.1
, the ground-truth community matrix
Z^{\circ}
is feasible for
Eq.

6.1
.
Hence, any pseudo-distribution satisfying these constraints obeys

\|ZZ^{\top}-M^{\circ}\|_{F}^{2}\leqslant 10\eta\,n^{2}/k+\frac{8\chi^{2}k\,n^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\,d}\,.

Standard rounding then yields a community matrix
M=\tilde{\E}ZZ^{T}\in[0,1]^{n\times n}
such that

\|M-M^{\circ}\|_{F}^{2}\leqslant\bigl(\frac{10\,\eta}{k}+\frac{8\,\chi^{2}\,k}{\varepsilon^{2}\,d}\bigr)\,n^{2}.

(6.2)

Having established the Frobenius-norm bound, the rest of the proof mirrors Lemma 7.3 of
[
38
]
; we present it below for completeness. Since the rows of
M
lie in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we can apply a Euclidean
k
-means algorithm with a
\gamma=7
approximation guarantee
[
27
]
.
Evaluating the
k
-means objective at the ground-truth partition
(S_{1},\ldots,S_{k})
shows that

k\text{-means}_{\text{opt}}(M)\ \leqslant\ \|M-M^{\circ}\|_{F}^{2}\ \leqslant\ \Bigl(\frac{10\,\eta}{k}+\frac{8\,\chi^{2}\,k}{\varepsilon^{2}\,d}\Bigr)\,n^{2},

where the last inequality is
Eq.

6.2
.

Now fix any alternative clustering
S^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,S^{\prime}_{k}
of the rows,
and consider the same clustering evaluated on
M^{\circ}
. For one cluster, say
S^{\prime}_{1}
, let

s_{b}=|S^{\prime}_{1}\cap S_{b}|\quad\text{ for }b\in[k].

As in Lemma 7.3 of
[
38
]
, the
k
-means objective contributed by
S^{\prime}_{1}
on the rows of
M^{\circ}
is at least

\Bigl(s_{1}+\cdots+s_{k}-\max\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{k}\}\Bigr)\cdot\frac{n}{4k}.

(6.3)

Summing (
6.3
) over all clusters
S^{\prime}_{a}
lower-bounds the
k
-means objective on
M^{\circ}
for the partition
S^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,S^{\prime}_{k}
.
Let the computed clustering be
S^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,S^{\prime}_{k}
.
Define

\delta=\frac{1}{n}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi:[k]\to[k]\\
\pi\ \text{invertible}\end{subarray}}\sum_{a=1}^{k}|S^{\prime}_{a}\setminus S_{\pi(a)}|,\qquad\delta^{\prime}=\frac{1}{n}\min_{f:[k]\to[k]}\sum_{a=1}^{k}|S^{\prime}_{a}\setminus S_{f(a)}|.

Here
\delta
is exactly the misclassification error of
S^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,S^{\prime}_{k}
relative to the ground truth.
As in
[
38
]
, one has
\delta\leqslant 2\delta^{\prime}
.
Moreover, using (
6.3
) and summing over clusters shows that the
k
-means objective of
S^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,S^{\prime}_{k}
on
M^{\circ}
is at least
\tfrac{n^{2}}{4k}\,\delta^{\prime}
.
Combining this lower bound with the
\gamma
-approximation guarantee on
M
and
Eq.

6.2

(exactly as in Lemma 7.3 of
[
38
]
) yields

\delta\leqslant 10^{4}\,k\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\eta}{k}+\frac{\chi^{2}\,k}{\varepsilon^{2}\,d}}}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which is the desired bound.
∎

7
Robustly identifying well-recovered clusters

In this section, we show that given a set of
n/k
vertices in the graph sampled from
\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
, we can decide between

•

Case I: at least 0.99 of the vertices in the set belong to the same community;

•

Case II: no more than 0.98 of the vertices in the set belong to any single community.

Restriction to the given set.

Let
S\subseteq[n]
denote the given set of
\lvert S\rvert=n/k
vertices, and let
\Ind=\mathbf{1}_{S}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
be its indicator.
We define the matrices restricted to
S
as

G_{s}\coloneqq G\odot(\Ind_{S}\Ind_{S}^{\top})\qquad\text{and}\qquad J_{s}\coloneqq\Ind_{S}\Ind_{S}^{\top}.

Throughout this section, every appearance of
G
and
J
inside operator norms or inner products is to be understood as
G_{s}
and
J_{s}
respectively. We also view all vectors (e.g.,
z,\tau,\zeta
) as elements of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
supported on
S
.

Theorem

(Restatement of
Theorem

5.3
)
.

Let
(G^{\circ},Z^{\circ})\sim\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
be generated from the
k
-stochastic block model and
G
be generated by adversarially corrupting
\eta
-fraction of the nodes in
G^{\circ}
.
Assume
k\leqslant n^{0.001}
,
\eta\leqslant\frac{1}{\poly(k)}
,
d=o(n)
, and
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant Kk^{2}
for some sufficiently large constant
K
.
For any set
S\subset V
of
n/k
vertices, there exists a polynomial-time algorithm that, with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-\tfrac{1}{\poly(n)}
, outputs

•

YES if at least
0.99
of the vertices in
S
belong to the same community;

•

NO if no more than
0.98
of the vertices in
S
belong to any single community.

We will show that the feasibility of the degree-
2
SoS relaxation of the following program satisfies
Theorem

5.3
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left.\begin{cases}z\odot z=z,\,\langle z,\Ind\rangle\geqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{0.99}{k}-\eta-\rho}}\right)n\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{\alpha/k}\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}}}\right\rangle\geqslant\frac{0.97(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}\end{cases}}}\right\}\,,

(7.1)

where
\alpha=d+(1-\frac{1}{k})\varepsilon d
,
\eta
is the corruption rate,
\chi
is the universal constant defined in the spectral result in
Theorem

B.1
, and
\rho=\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-2C_{d,\varepsilon}}}\right)
is the fraction of high
-
degree vertices that need to be trimmed to obtain the spectral norm bound (see
Corollary

B.2
).

Lemma 7.1
.

Given a set of
n/k
vertices in the graph sampled from
\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
where at least 0.99 fraction of the vertices in the set belongs to the same community (Case I), when
\eta\leqslant C_{\eta}
for some constant
C_{\eta}
that is small enough, Program
Eq.

7.1
is feasible with probability at least
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{n}
(supported on
S
) be the indicator vector for the largest set of uncorrupted and degree-bounded nodes (according to
Theorem

B.1
) that are in the same community. We will show that, with probability
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
,
\tau
is a feasible solution to the program
Eq.

7.1
.

By assumption,
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}\geqslant(\frac{0.99}{k}-\eta-\rho)n
. Therefore,
\tau
satisfies the integrality and norm constraints of program
Eq.

7.1
.

By
Theorem

B.1
, with probability at least
1-\frac{k^{2}}{n^{2}}
, we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{k}}
. Therefore,
\tau
satisfies the spectral constraint of program
Eq.

7.1
.

Now, consider
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle
. Notice that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle G_{s},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle
is equal to
2
times the number of edges in the induced subgraph
G_{s}(\tau)
where each edge is sampled independently with probability
\frac{\alpha}{n}
. Therefore, by Chernoff bound, it follows that, with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle G_{s},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle\geqslant\frac{\alpha}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}^{2}-O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\frac{\alpha\log(n)}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}^{2}}}}\right)\,.

Hence,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle\geqslant\frac{\alpha-d}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}^{2}-O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\frac{\alpha\log(n)}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}^{2}}}}\right)\,.

Since
\alpha=d+(1-\frac{1}{k})\varepsilon d
,
\alpha\leqslant 2d
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}\geqslant(\frac{0.99}{k}-\eta-\rho)n
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{0.98(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}-O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{d\log(n)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{0.975(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}\,.

Thus,
\tau
satisfies the sum constraint of program
Eq.

7.1
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Given a set of
n/k
vertices in the graph sampled from
\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
where at most a 0.98 fraction of the vertices in the set belongs to the same community (Case II), when
\eta\leqslant C_{\eta}
for some constant
C_{\eta}
that is small enough, Program
Eq.

7.1
is not feasible with probability at least
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

Proof.

We will show that there is an SoS proof of

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.963(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}\,,

which is a refutation to constraint
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}}}\right\rangle\geqslant\frac{0.97(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}
in
\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)
.

Let us denote the set of uncorrupted and degree-bounded (according to
Corollary

B.2
) nodes by
\zeta\in\{0,1\}^{n}
(supported on
S
).
By
Corollary

B.2
, it follows that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta}}\right\rVert_{1}\geqslant(1-\eta-\rho)\frac{n}{k}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((G_{0})_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d/k}
, where
(G_{0})_{s}\coloneqq G_{0}\odot(\Ind\Ind^{\top})
.
Consider the largest set of vertices in
\zeta
that are from the same community, denoted by
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{n}
(supported on
S
).
By our assumption, it follows that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}\leqslant 0.98\frac{n}{k}
.

We will decompose
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}}}\right\rangle
into the following three terms and bound them separately

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}}}\right\rangle=&\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}+(\zeta-\tau)(\zeta-\tau)^{\top}}}\right\rangle\\
+&\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},2(\zeta-\tau)\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle\,.\end{split}

(7.2)

Term 1

For the first term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}+(\zeta-\tau)(\zeta-\tau)^{\top}}}\right\rangle
, we can further decompose it into the following two terms

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}+(\zeta-\tau)(\zeta-\tau)^{\top}}}\right\rangle=

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},(\zeta-\tau)(\zeta-\tau)^{\top}}}\right\rangle\,.

The two terms can be bounded using the same method. Consider the first term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\frac{\alpha-d}{n}J_{s}\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle+\frac{\alpha-d}{n}\langle\tau,z\rangle^{2}\,.

Since
\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}
certifies
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{\alpha/k}
, it follows that

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\chi\sqrt{\alpha/k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}+\frac{\alpha-d}{n}\langle\tau,z\rangle^{2}\,.

Using the same analysis by replacing
\tau
by
\zeta-\tau
, we can also obtain

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},(\zeta-\tau)(\zeta-\tau)^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\chi\sqrt{\alpha/k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta-\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}+\frac{\alpha-d}{n}\langle\zeta-\tau,z\rangle^{2}\,.

Combining the two inequalities, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}&\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}+(\zeta-\tau)(\zeta-\tau)^{\top}}}\right\rangle\\
&\leqslant\frac{\alpha-d}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\langle\tau,z\rangle^{2}+\langle\zeta-\tau,z\rangle^{2}}}\right)+\chi\sqrt{\alpha/k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta-\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(7.3)

Since
\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}0\leqslant z\leqslant 1
, it follows that

\langle\tau,z\rangle\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}
and
\langle\zeta-\tau,z\rangle\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta-\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}
.
Moreover, since
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}\leqslant 0.98\frac{n}{k}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta}}\right\rVert_{1}\leqslant\frac{n}{k}
, the value of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta-\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}^{2}
is upper bounded by
(0.98\frac{n}{k})^{2}+(0.02\frac{n}{k})^{2}\leqslant 0.961\frac{n^{2}}{k^{2}}
.
Therefore, we can obtain

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\langle\tau,z\rangle^{2}+\langle\zeta-\tau,z\rangle^{2}\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta-\tau}}\right\rVert_{1}^{2}\leqslant 0.961\frac{n^{2}}{k^{2}}\,.

(7.4)

Since
\tau
and
\zeta
are Boolean vectors and
\tau\subseteq\zeta
, it follows that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta-\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant\frac{n}{k}\,.

(7.5)

Plugging
Eq.

7.4
and
Eq.

7.5
into
Eq.

7.3
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}+(\zeta-\tau)(\zeta-\tau)^{\top}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{0.961(\alpha-d)n}{k^{2}}+\frac{\chi\sqrt{\alpha}n}{\sqrt{k^{3}}}\,.

Plugging in
\alpha=d+(1-\frac{1}{k})\varepsilon d
and
\alpha\leqslant 2d
, we get

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\Bigl(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}\Bigr)\odot zz^{\top},\tau\tau^{\top}+(\zeta-\tau)(\zeta-\tau)^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.961(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}+\frac{2\chi\sqrt{d}n}{\sqrt{k^{3}}}\,.

(7.6)

Term 2

For the the second term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},2(\zeta-\tau)\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle
, since we are summing over entries in the set of uncorrupted vertices
\zeta
, it follows that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},2(\zeta-\tau)\tau^{\top}}}\right\rangle=2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((G_{0})_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot\zeta\zeta^{\top},(\zeta-\tau)\tau^{\top}\odot zz^{\top}}}\right\rangle\,.

To simplify notation, let us denote
M_{d}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((G_{0})_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot\zeta\zeta^{\top}
. It follows by SoS Cauchy-Schwarz (
A.3
) that

\displaystyle 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle M_{d},(\zeta-\tau)\tau^{\top}\odot zz^{\top}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle M_{d}\cdot(\tau\odot z),(\zeta-\tau)\odot z}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert M_{d}\cdot(\tau\odot z)}}\right\rVert^{2}}{\chi\sqrt{d/k}}+\chi\sqrt{d/k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert(\zeta-\tau)\odot z}}\right\rVert^{2}\,.

Since we chose
\zeta
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert M_{d}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d/k}
, it follows that

2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle M_{d},(\zeta-\tau)\tau^{\top}\odot zz^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d/k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert(\zeta-\tau)\odot z}}\right\rVert^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau\odot z}}\right\rVert^{2}}}\right)\,.

Since
\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}0\leqslant z\leqslant 1
, it follows that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert(\zeta-\tau)\odot z}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta-\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau\odot z}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}
. Moreover, since
\zeta
and
\tau
are Boolean, we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta-\tau}}\right\rVert^{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant\frac{n}{k}
. Hence, we can obtain

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2(\zeta-\tau)\tau^{\top}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{\chi\sqrt{d}n}{\sqrt{k^{3}}}\,.

(7.7)

Term 3

For the third term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle
, we can further decompose it into

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\alpha-d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle\,.

(7.8)

Since
\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}
certifies
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\chi\sqrt{\alpha/k}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\zeta}}\right\rVert_{1}\geqslant(1-\eta-\rho)\frac{n}{k}
, we can apply Frobenius Cauchy–Schwarz and get

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{2\chi\sqrt{\eta+\rho}\,\sqrt{\alpha}n}{\sqrt{k^{3}}}\,.

Since
\alpha\leqslant 2d
,
\rho\ll 1
(due to
Corollary

B.2
) and the assumption that
\eta\ll 1
, it follows that

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{\alpha}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{\chi\sqrt{d}n}{\sqrt{k^{3}}}\,.

(7.9)

Since
\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}0\leqslant z\leqslant 1
and
\alpha=d+(1-\frac{1}{k})\varepsilon d
, it follows that

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\alpha-d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{2(k-1)(\eta+\rho)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}\,.

Since
\rho\ll 1
(due to
Corollary

B.2
) and the assumption that
\eta\ll 1
, it follows that

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\alpha-d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.001(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}\,.

(7.10)

Plugging
Eq.

7.9
and
Eq.

7.10
into
Eq.

7.8
, it follows that

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}-\zeta\zeta^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.001(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}+\frac{\chi\sqrt{d}n}{\sqrt{k^{3}}}\,.

(7.11)

Conclusion

Plugging
Eq.

7.6
,
Eq.

7.7
and
Eq.

7.11
into
Eq.

7.2
, we can obtain

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.962(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}+\frac{4\chi\sqrt{d}n}{\sqrt{k^{3}}}\,.

Since
k^{2}\ll\varepsilon^{2}d
, we can obtain

\frac{4\chi\sqrt{d}n}{\sqrt{k^{3}}}\leqslant\frac{0.00001}{\sqrt{k}}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon dn}{k^{2}}\leqslant\frac{0.001(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}\,.

Thus, it follows that

\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.10762pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.10762pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.10762pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{z}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.963(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}\,.

This is a refutation to constraint
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{s}-\frac{d}{n}J_{s}}}\right)\odot zz^{\top},\Ind\Ind^{\top}}}\right\rangle\geqslant\frac{0.97(k-1)\varepsilon dn}{k^{3}}
in
\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

5.3
.

By
Lemma

7.1
and
Lemma

7.2
, it follows that, given a set of
n/k
vertices in the graph sampled from
\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
, we can check feasibility of
\mathcal{A}_{\text{verify}}(z;G,\eta)
(interpreted with
G_{s},J_{s}
as above) in time
n^{O(1)}
to decide whether the set of vertices is in Case I or Case II:

•

Case I (at least
0.99
of the vertices in the set belong to the same community): feasibility follows by
Lemma

7.1
. Since the SoS SDP has small bit complexity and there exists a Boolean solution, by arguments analogous to those in
[
46
]
, the ellipsoid method will be able to find a feasible solution in time
n^{O(1)}
.

•

Case II (no more than
0.98
of the vertices in the set belong to any single community):
By
Lemma

7.2
, the SoS program is infeasible; hence no feasible solution exists (and, in particular, none can be found in time
n^{O(1)}
).

The success probability is
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
.
∎

8
Robust boosting algorithm for bisection

In this section, we prove
Theorem

5.4
for the robust bisection algorithm in the
k
-stochastic block model. The procedure essentially constitutes recovery in the
2
-SBM model by aggregating communities into two global groups, each comprising
k/2
local communities.

8.1
Algorithm and constraint systems

We first introduce the polynomial constraints that we use in the algorithm.

(i) Labeling constraints.
The feasible label variable
Z\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
satisfies

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{label}}(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{Z(i,a)^{2}=Z(i,a),\penalty\ \sum_{a}Z(i,a)=1,\penalty\ \sum_{i}Z(i,a)=\tfrac{n}{k}}}\right\}\,.

(8.1)

(ii) Initialization.
Let
Z_{\mathrm{init}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
be an initializer
0.001
-approximation error and recovers
0.99
fraction of the vertices in communities
a\in[k/2]
, we add constraints to enforce the SoS variable
Z
is close to
Z_{\mathrm{init}}
,

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{init}}(Z;Z_{\mathrm{init}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left.\begin{cases}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert Z(\cdot,a)-Z_{\mathrm{init}}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant 0.001n/k\quad\forall a\in[k/2]\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z(\cdot,a)-\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z_{\mathrm{init}}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant 0.001n\end{cases}}}\right\}\,.

(8.2)

(iii) Corruption mask / node distance.

We model node corruptions through a binary mask
\xi\in\{0,1\}^{n}
and require the program matrix to agree with the observed graph outside the corrupted rows/columns:

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{close}}(Y,\xi;\eta,\bar{G})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(Y-\bar{G})\odot(\mathbf{1}-\xi)(\mathbf{1}-\xi)^{\top}=0,\xi\odot\xi=\xi,\sum_{i}\xi_{i}\leqslant\delta_{\eta}n}}\right\}\,,

(8.3)

where
\delta_{\eta}=\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon})+\eta
.

(iv) Community structure and spectral condition.

We encode the decomposition of the centered adjacency into pairwise components and bound the spectral norm of the noise blocks:

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{mix}}(Y,Z)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{X=ZZ^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}J,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert Y-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}X}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\chi+\frac{1}{k}}}\right)\sqrt{d}}}\right\}\,.

(8.4)

(v) Majority-vote consistency.

The final constraint system characterizes the constraints for the consistency of bisection majority voting, i.e. enforcing that the induced bisection of
X
(bisection of vertices in community
[\frac{k}{2}]
and
(\frac{k}{2},k]
) is consistent with the concentration result for majority voting:

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{maj}}(Y,Z,R;\gamma,\tilde{C})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{R\colon\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{set}}(z)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.728pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Yx(Z),x(Z)\odot z}}\right\rangle\geqslant\alpha_{\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i}z_{i}-\beta_{\gamma,\tilde{C}}n}}\right)}}\right\}\,,

(8.5)

where
\alpha_{\gamma}=\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{16k}
,
\beta_{\gamma,\tilde{C}}=\frac{640k}{1-\gamma}\exp(-\gamma\tilde{C}/2)
,
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{set}}(z)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{z\odot z=z\}
, and
x(Z)\coloneqq\displaystyle 2\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z(\cdot,a)-\mathbf{1}

13
13

13

We write
x
for simplicity when
Z
is clear from context
.

For our algorithm, we define polynomial system
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi,R;\bar{G},Z_{\mathrm{init}},\eta,\gamma,\tilde{C})
based on the set of constraints defined above
14
14

14

For simplicity, we use
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi)
to denote
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi,R;\bar{G},Z_{\mathrm{init}},\eta,\gamma,\tilde{C})
when the input is clear from the context.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi,R;\bar{G},Z_{\mathrm{init}},\eta,\gamma,\tilde{C})\\
&\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{label}}(Z)\cup\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{init}}(Z;Z_{\mathrm{init}})\cup\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{close}}(Y,\xi;\eta,\bar{G})\cup\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{mix}}(Y,Z,\xi)\cup\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{maj}}(Y,Z,R;\gamma,\tilde{C})\,.\end{split}

(8.6)

Algorithm.

Now we describe our algorithm in
Algorithm

8.1
.

Algorithm 8.1

(Robust bisection boosting algorithm)
.

Input:
The graph
G
sampled from the
k
-stochastic block model
\mathsf{SBM}_{n}(d,\varepsilon,k)
with
\eta n
corrupted nodes, and initial clusters
S_{1},S_{2},\ldots,S_{k/2}\subseteq[n]
with its corresponding label matrix
Z_{\mathrm{init}}
.

1.

Finding degree-
O(1)
pseudo-distribution satisfying the polynomial constraints
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi,R;\bar{G},Z_{\mathrm{init}},\eta,\gamma,\tilde{C})

where
\bar{G}=G-\frac{d}{n}J
is the centered adjacency matrix,
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}
and
\tilde{C}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{a^{\frac{2}{k}}b^{1-\frac{2}{k}}}-\sqrt{b}}}\right)^{2}
.

2.

Rounding the pseudo-distribution by sign to obtain the bisection
\hat{x}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\pm 1}}\right\}^{n}
from
x(Z)=2\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z(\cdot,a)-\mathbf{1}
.

8.2
Feasibility and time complexity

We first show that the program is feasible with high probability and runs in polynomial time.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
\bar{G}^{\circ}
be the centered adjacency matrix of the uncorrupted graph and
S
be the set of nodes with degree larger than
20d
.
Let
T
be the set of corrupted nodes.
Let
Z^{\circ}
be the ground truth label matrix.
Under the setting of
Algorithm

8.1
, program
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi)
is satisfied by
(\bar{G}^{\circ}\odot(\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}^{\top}),Z^{\circ},\mathbf{1}_{S\cup T})
with probability at least
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}-\exp(-\Omega(k))
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{label}}(Z^{\circ})
and
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{init}}(Z;Z_{\mathrm{init}})
is satisfied by definition. Let
X^{\circ}=Z^{\circ}(Z^{\circ})^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}J
.
By
Corollary

B.2
, the size of set
S
is bounded by
\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon})\,n
and the spectral norm of
\bar{G}^{\circ}-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}X^{\circ}
is bounded by
\chi\sqrt{d}
with high probability. Notice that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\bar{G}^{\circ}\odot(\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}^{\top})-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}X^{\circ}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bar{G}^{\circ}-\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}X^{\circ}}}\right)\odot(\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}^{\top})}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert X^{\circ}\odot(J-\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}^{\top})}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d}+\frac{\varepsilon d}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert X^{\circ}\odot(J-\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}^{\top})}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d}+\frac{2\varepsilon d}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert X^{\circ}\odot(\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}_{S}^{\top})}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\chi\sqrt{d}+\frac{4\varepsilon\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{k}}\exp(-C_{d,\varepsilon})\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle=\chi\sqrt{d}+4C_{d,\varepsilon}\exp(-C_{d,\varepsilon})\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\chi+\frac{1}{k}}}\right)\sqrt{d}\,.

Therefore,
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{mix}}(\bar{G}^{\circ}\odot(\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}^{\top}),Z^{\circ})
is feasible with probability
1-n^{-O(1)}
. Moreover,
\lVert\mathbf{1}_{S\cup T}\rVert_{1}\leqslant|S|+|T|\leqslant\delta_{\eta}n
with probability
1-n^{-O(1)}
. Hence,
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{close}}(\bar{G}^{\circ}\odot(\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}^{\top}),\mathbf{1}_{S\cup T};\eta,\bar{G})
is feasible with probability
1-n^{-O(1)}
.

By
Corollary

4.4
, the voting lower bound in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{maj}}(\bar{G}^{\circ}\odot(\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}\mathbf{1}_{\bar{S}}^{\top}),Z^{\circ},R;\gamma,\tilde{C})
is feasible for
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}
and
\tilde{C}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{a^{\frac{2}{k}}b^{1-\frac{2}{k}}}-\sqrt{b}}}\right)^{2}
with probability
1-\exp(-\Omega(k))-n^{-\Omega(1)}
. The existence of the SoS proof of
Theorem

4.3
follows by
Theorem

A.6
and
Lemma

A.7
.

The feasibility proof is completed by a union bound over all failure probabilities.
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

Algorithm

8.1
runs in polynomial time.

Proof.

Since the sum-of-squares relaxation is at a constant degree, the resulting semidefinite program involves only polynomially many constraints and can be solved in polynomial time.
∎

8.3
SoS guarantees for robust bisection boosting

Now, we show that
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi)
boosts the bisection error rate from
0.001
to
1/\poly(k)
.
To do this, we consider two feasible solutions
(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)})
and
(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)})
of
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi)
.
We will show that the constraints in
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi)
allow us to prove that any two good solutions will be close to each other in the bisection sense.
Since
Z^{\circ}
will also be a good solution, this implies that any feasible solution of
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi)
will be close to
Z^{\circ}
in the bisection sense.

Throughout this section, we define the following terms:

•

Let
x^{(1)}=2\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(1)}(\cdot,a)-\mathbf{1}
and
x^{(2)}=2\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(2)}(\cdot,a)-\mathbf{1}
be the induced bisections of
Z^{(1)}
and
Z^{(2)}
.

•

Let
v=\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}\odot x^{(2)}}{2}
be the set of nodes where the bisections
x^{(1)}
and
x^{(2)}
differ.

•

Let
s=\mathbf{1}-(\mathbf{1}-\xi^{(1)})\odot(\mathbf{1}-\xi^{(2)})
be the set of nodes where
Y^{(1)}
and
Y^{(2)}
differ.

•

Let
w=v\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)
be the set of vertices in the shared part of
Y^{(1)}
and
Y^{(2)}
such that the bisections
x^{(1)}
and
x^{(2)}
differ.

•

Let
g=\mathbf{1}-(\mathbf{1}-v)\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)
be union of
v
and
s
, notice that, equivalently
g=w+s
.

To prove the algorithmic guarantee of
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z,\xi)
, we need the following observations on properties of the variables defined above.

Lemma 8.4
.

x^{(1)}
,
x^{(2)}
and
v
satisfies

x^{(1)}\odot v=-x^{(2)}\odot v\quad\text{and}\quad x^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)=x^{(2)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)\,,

and,

v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}}{2}=v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}+x^{(2)}}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(2)}}{2}=v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}+x^{(1)}}{2}\,.

Proof.

By the definition of
v=\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}\odot x^{(2)}}{2}
, it follows that

x^{(1)}\odot v=\frac{x^{(1)}-x^{(2)}}{2}=-x^{(2)}\odot v\,,

and,

x^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)=\frac{x^{(1)}+x^{(2)}}{2}=x^{(2)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)\,.

Now, we show
v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}}{2}=v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}+x^{(2)}}{2}
, and the other side follows by symmetry. By plugging in
v=\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}\odot x^{(2)}}{2}
, the left hand side is

v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}}{2}=\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}-x^{(1)}\odot x^{(2)}+x^{(2)}}{4}\,,

and the right hand side is

v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}+x^{(2)}}{2}=\frac{\mathbf{1}+x^{(2)}-x^{(1)}\odot x^{(2)}-x^{(1)}}{4}\,.

Therefore, we have
v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}}{2}=v\odot\frac{\mathbf{1}+x^{(2)}}{2}
.
∎

Lemma 8.5
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{103.23824pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)},Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(1)}(x^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-g)),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle=-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(2)}(x^{(2)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-g)),x^{(2)}\odot w}}\right\rangle\,.

Proof.

Plugging in the definition of
g
and
w
, the left hand side can be written as

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(1)}(x^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-g)),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(1)}(x^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)),x^{(1)}\odot v\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}}}\right)(x^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)),x^{(1)}\odot v}}\right\rangle\,.

By
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{close}}(Y,\xi;\eta,\bar{G})
, we have

Y^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}=\bar{G}\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}=Y^{(2)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}\,,

and, by
Lemma

8.4
, we have

x^{(1)}\odot v=-x^{(2)}\odot v\quad\text{and}\quad x^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)=x^{(2)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)\,,

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(1)}(x^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-g)),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}}}\right)(x^{(2)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-v)),x^{(2)}\odot v}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(2)}(x^{(2)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-g)),x^{(2)}\odot w}}\right\rangle\,.

∎

Lemma 8.6
.

The norm of
v
,
s
and
g
satisfies the following SoS inequalities

\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{36.78072pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-36.78072pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[36.78072pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-36.78072pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[36.78072pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Z^{(1)},Z^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert v}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant 0.004n\,,

and,

\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{32.18074pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-32.18074pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[32.18074pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-32.18074pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[32.18074pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert s}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant 2\delta_{\eta}n\,,

and,

\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{67.22542pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-67.22542pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[67.22542pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-67.22542pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[67.22542pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Z^{(1)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert w}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant 0.004n\,,

and, for any
b\in[\frac{k}{2}]
and
t\in\{1,2\}
,

\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{67.22542pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-67.22542pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[67.22542pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-67.22542pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[67.22542pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Z^{(1)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Z^{(t)}(\cdot,b),w}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.004n}{k}\,.

Proof.

For the bound on
v
, by constraints in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{init}}(Z;Z_{\mathrm{init}})
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert v}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\frac{\mathbf{1}-x^{(1)}\odot x^{(2)}}{2}}}\right\rVert^{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\frac{x^{(1)}-x^{(2)}}{2}}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(1)}(\cdot,a)-\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(2)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(1)}(\cdot,a)-\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z_{\mathrm{init}}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rVert^{2}+2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(2)}(\cdot,a)-\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z_{\mathrm{init}}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant 0.004n\,.

For the bound on
s
, by constraints in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{close}}(Y,\xi;\eta,\bar{G})
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert s}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}s_{i}=\sum_{i}1-(1-\xi^{(1)}_{i})\odot(1-\xi^{(2)}_{i})=\sum_{i}\xi^{(1)}_{i}+\sum_{i}\xi^{(2)}_{i}(1-\xi^{(1)}_{i})

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i}\xi^{(1)}_{i}+\sum_{i}\xi^{(2)}_{i}\leqslant 2\delta_{\eta}n\,.

Now, consider
w=v\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)
. Therefore,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert w}}\right\rVert^{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert v\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert v}}\right\rVert^{2}\leqslant 0.004n\,.

For the last inequality, for
b\in[\frac{k}{2}]
, it follows that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Z^{(1)}(\cdot,b),w}}\right\rangle=\sum_{i}Z^{(1)}(i,b)v_{i}(1-s_{i})\leqslant\sum_{i}Z^{(1)}(i,b)v_{i}\,.

Since
v=x^{(1)}\odot\Big(\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(1)}(\cdot,a)-\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(2)}(\cdot,a)\Big)
and
x^{(1)}\odot Z^{(1)}(\cdot,b)=Z^{(1)}(\cdot,b)
for
b\in[\frac{k}{2}]
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i}Z^{(1)}(i,b)v_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}Z^{(1)}(i,b)x^{(1)}_{i}\Big(\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(1)}(i,a)-\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(2)}(i,a)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}Z^{(1)}(i,b)-\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(2)}(i,a)Z^{(1)}(i,b)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i}Z^{(1)}(i,b)-Z^{(2)}(i,b)Z^{(1)}(i,b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}Z^{(1)}(i,b)\big(1-Z^{(2)}(i,b)\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i}\big(Z^{(1)}(i,b)-Z^{(2)}(i,b)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert Z^{(1)}(\cdot,b)-Z^{(2)}(\cdot,b)}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert Z^{(1)}(\cdot,b)-Z_{\mathrm{init}}(\cdot,b)}}\right\rVert^{2}+2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert Z^{(2)}(\cdot,b)-Z_{\mathrm{init}}(\cdot,b)}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{0.004\,n}{k}\,.

Thus,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Z^{(1)}(\cdot,b),w}}\right\rangle\leqslant\sum_{i}Z^{(1)}(i,b)v_{i}\leqslant\frac{0.004\,n}{k}\,.

∎

Lemma 8.7
.

For any
a\in[\frac{k}{2}]
and
t\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{103.23824pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)},Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.008n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\,,

and,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{103.23824pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)},Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\geqslant-\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\,.

Proof.

Recall that
X^{(t)}=Z^{(t)}(Z^{(t)})^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}J
. We consider
Z^{(t)}(Z^{(t)})^{\top}
and
\frac{1}{k}J
separately. For the
Z^{(t)}(Z^{(t)})^{\top}
part, notice that, since
x_{i}=2\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z(i,a)-1
, we have
x_{i}^{(t)}\cdot Z^{(t)}(i,a)=Z^{(t)}(i,a)
for
a\in[k/2]
. Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Z^{(t)}(Z^{(t)})^{\top}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in[k]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Z^{(t)}(\cdot,b)Z^{(t)}(\cdot,b)^{\top}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in[k]}Z^{(t)}(\cdot,b)^{\top}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right)Z^{(t)}(\cdot,b)^{\top}(x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a))

\displaystyle=Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)^{\top}(x^{(t)}\odot w)Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)^{\top}(x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a))

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a),w}}\right\rangle Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)^{\top}(w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a))

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\,,

where the last inequality is by
Lemma

8.6
. Moreover, from the last equality, we can also obtain

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Z^{(t)}(Z^{(t)})^{\top}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\geqslant 0

For the
\frac{1}{k}J
part, we have

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\frac{1}{k}J\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathbf{1},x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right\rangle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathbf{1},x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathbf{1},w}}\right\rangle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\,.

Equivalently, we can obtain

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\frac{1}{k}J\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\geqslant-\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\,.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Z^{(t)}(Z^{(t)})^{\top}-\frac{1}{k}J}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{0.008n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\,,

and,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\geqslant-\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\,.

∎

Corollary 8.8
.

For any
t\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{103.23824pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)},Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot\frac{x^{(t)}+\mathbf{1}}{2}}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.008n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert w}}\right\rVert^{2}\,,

and,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{103.23824pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)},Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot\frac{x^{(t)}+\mathbf{1}}{2}}}\right\rangle\geqslant-\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert w}}\right\rVert^{2}\,.

Proof.

Recall that
x^{(t)}=2\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)-\mathbf{1}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot\frac{(x^{(t)}+\mathbf{1})}{2}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot\sum_{a\in[k/2]}Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in[k/2]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle\,.

Applying
Lemma

8.7
, we can get

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot\frac{(x^{(t)}+\mathbf{1})}{2}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{a\in[k/2]}\frac{0.008n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{0.008n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,\frac{x^{(t)}+\mathbf{1}}{2}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{0.008n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert w}}\right\rVert^{2}\,,

and,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle X^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w\odot\frac{(x^{(t)}+\mathbf{1})}{2}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant-\sum_{a\in[k/2]}\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,Z^{(t)}(\cdot,a)}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle w,\frac{x^{(t)}+\mathbf{1}}{2}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant-\frac{0.004n}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert w}}\right\rVert^{2}\,.

∎

The following bound on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot g}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right\rangle
will be the key thing that we need in the proof of the algorithmic guarantees.

Lemma 8.9
.

For
t\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)}}}\right),\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{103.23824pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-103.23824pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[103.23824pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y^{(1)},Z^{(1)},\xi^{(1)},Y^{(2)},Z^{(2)},\xi^{(2)}}$}}}$}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(t)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(t)}\odot g}}\right),x^{(t)}\odot w}}\right\rangle\leqslant\frac{0.016\varepsilon d}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert w}}\right\rVert^{2}+4\varepsilon d\delta_{\eta}n\,.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we prove the statement for
t=1
. Let
Y^{(1)}_{S}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=Y^{(1)}\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}
, we use decomposition

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot g}}\right),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)}-Y^{(1)}_{S}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot g}}\right),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle Y^{(1)}_{S}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot g}}\right),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle\,.

(8.7)

For the term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)}-Y^{(1)}_{S}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot g}}\right),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle
, since
Y^{(1)}-Y^{(1)}_{S}=Y^{(1)}\odot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(J-(\mathbf{1}-s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}}}\right)
,
g=w+s
, and
w=v\odot(\mathbf{1}-s)
, we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)}-Y^{(1)}_{S}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle=0
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(J-(\mathbf{1}-s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}}}\right)\odot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot s}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right)^{\top}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot s}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right)^{\top}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)}-Y^{(1)}_{S}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot g}}\right),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle=

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y^{(1)}-Y^{(1)}_{S}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x^{(1)}\odot s}}\right),x^{(1)}\odot w}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=
