z=\mathbf{1}_{S}
for some
S\subseteq\{j:\,y_{j}\neq 0\}
with
|S|=\rho\,n_{i}

(if
\rho n_{i}\notin\mathbb{N}
, take
\lfloor\rho n_{i}\rfloor
and
\lceil\rho n_{i}\rceil
;
any other
z\in[0,1]^{n}
with the same
\ell_{1}
-norm is a convex combination).

Let us first construct an auxiliary matrix
G^{\mathrm{ind}}
in which, for each fixed row
u
, the variables
\bm{\{}G^{\mathrm{ind}}_{uv}\}_{v}
are independent Bernoullis with the same means as above (and different rows are also independent); define
\bm{X^{\mathrm{ind}}_{u}}
analogously. Then

\langle G^{\mathrm{ind}}y\odot y,\,\mathbf{1}_{S}\rangle=\sum_{u\in S}\bm{X^{\mathrm{ind}}_{u}}.

We now apply
Theorem

C.4
to the sum
\displaystyle\sum_{u\in S}\bm{X^{\mathrm{ind}}_{u}}
by independence across rows with the parameter

\widetilde{\theta}_{i}=2\rho n_{i}\frac{\beta_{i}\widetilde{C}_{i}-\log(1/\rho)-3t}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}=2\rho n_{i}\frac{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}.

This yields

\displaystyle\ProbOp\Big[\sum_{u\in S}\bm{X^{\mathrm{ind}}_{u}}\leqslant\widetilde{\theta}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle\leqslant\exp\Big(-|S|\,\beta_{i}\widetilde{C}_{i}+\tfrac{\widetilde{\theta}_{i}}{2}\log\widetilde{R}_{i}\Big)=\exp\Big(-|S|(\log(1/\rho)+3t)\Big)\kern 5.0pt.

A union bound over all
S\subseteq\{y\neq 0\}
with
|S|=\rho n_{i}
gives

\ProbOp\Big[\exists\,S:\,\sum_{u\in S}\bm{X^{\mathrm{ind}}_{u}}\leqslant\widetilde{\theta}_{i}\Big]\leqslant\binom{n_{i}}{\rho n_{i}}\,e^{-|S|(\log(1/\rho)+3t)}\leqslant e^{-t\rho n_{i}},

using
\binom{n_{i}}{\rho n_{i}}\leqslant\exp(\rho n_{i}(\log(1/\rho)+1))
and
t\geqslant 1
.
Therefore, with probability at least
1-e^{-t\rho n_{i}}
, simultaneously for all such
S
,

\sum_{u\in S}\bm{X^{\mathrm{ind}}_{u}}\geqslant\widetilde{\theta}_{i}=2|S|\,\frac{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}.

(C.4)

It remains to pass from the independent sampling used above to the actual symmetric SBM with zero diagonal.
We, first, note that zeroing the diagonal affects the sum by at most
|S|=\rho n_{i}
.
We, further, note that only the block
S\times S
differs between the two sampling procedures.
Let
\Delta(S)
be the difference between the contributions of
S\times S
to

\sum_{u\in S}\bm{X^{\mathrm{ind}}_{u}}
and to
\sum_{u\in S}\bm{X_{u}}
.
As in
[
38
]
,
\Delta(S)
is a sum/difference of independent, mean-
0
, bounded variables,
each supported in
[-1,1]
and nonzero with probability at most
a/n

(so
\sigma^{2}\leqslant(a+b)/n
). Apply
Theorem

C.6
with
n_{1}=n_{2}=|S|
and

\sigma^{2}=(a+b)/n
(hence
\sigma\geqslant 20/\sqrt{n}
because
a\geqslant 400
). Since
i\geqslant 1
, we have
n_{i}\leqslant n
and hence
|S|=\rho n_{i}\leqslant\rho n\leqslant 10^{-6}n
, so the conditions hold. We obtain

\ProbOp\Big[|\Delta(S)|>2|S|\sqrt{a+b}\Big]\leqslant\exp\Big(-16|S|\log\big(\tfrac{n}{|S|}\big)\Big)\kern 5.0pt.

A union bound over all
\binom{n_{i}}{\rho n_{i}}
choices of
S
shows that, with additional failure
probability at most
1/n_{i}^{3}
(for all large
n
), we have
|\Delta(S)|\leqslant 2|S|\sqrt{a+b}
simultaneously for all such
S
.

Finally, intersecting the two high
-
probability events and noting that the level
-
i
bisection is balanced so we get

\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,\,\mathbf{1}_{S}\rangle=\sum_{u\in S}y_{u}\sum_{v}y_{v}\,(G_{uv}-d_{i})=\sum_{u\in S}\bm{X_{u}}\geqslant\widetilde{\theta}_{i}-2|S|\sqrt{a+b}.

Substituting
|S|=\rho n_{i}
and the definition of
\widetilde{\theta}_{i}
yields the desired conclusion.
∎

Theorem C.9
.

Fix a level
i\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lfloor\log_{2}k\rfloor\}

and set
\beta_{i}\coloneqq 2^{-i}
,
n_{i}\coloneqq 2\beta_{i}n
,
and
\gamma_{i}\coloneqq\alpha_{i}/\beta_{i}=2^{i}/k
with
\alpha_{i}=1/k
.
Assume
0<\varepsilon<1
and
k\geqslant 2
, and take other parameters as in
Theorem

C.4
.
Define

\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\min\Bigl(\,\frac{1}{e},\exp\bigl(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}d}{8k}\bigr)\Bigr)\kern 5.0pt.

If, in addition,
\varepsilon^{2}d\geqslant 9k^{2}
,
then for any
0<\rho\leqslant\min\{\rho_{\gamma},10^{-6}\}
and
t=\varepsilon^{2}d/k
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t\rho n_{i}}-\frac{1}{n_{i}^{3}}
we have

\langle Gy\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant-12\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d\qquad\text{for every }z\text{ with }\|z\|_{1}=\rho n_{i},

where
y\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
is any valid bisection labeling at level
i
.

Proof.

Let
t=\varepsilon^{2}d/k
and fix any
0<\rho\leqslant\min\{\rho_{\gamma},10^{-6}\}
.
By
Theorem

C.8
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t\rho n_{i}}-\frac{1}{n_{i}^{3}}
,
we have for all
z
with
\|z\|_{1}=\rho n_{i}
,

\langle Gy\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant 2\rho n_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\beta_{i}\widetilde{C}_{i}-\log(1/\rho)-3t}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}-\sqrt{a+b}}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(C.5)

We now bound the three potentially negative contributions on the right-hand side of (
C.5
).
For the
-\log(1/\rho)
term,
Lemma

C.2
yields

-\frac{2\rho n_{i}}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}\log\frac{1}{\rho}\geqslant-\frac{4\rho n_{i}}{\gamma_{i}\varepsilon}\log\frac{1}{\rho}\kern 5.0pt.

By the monotonicity of
x\log(1/x)
on
(0,e^{-1}]
and
\rho\leqslant\rho_{\gamma}\leqslant e^{-1}
, we have

\rho\log\frac{1}{\rho}\leqslant\rho_{\gamma}\log\frac{1}{\rho_{\gamma}},

and therefore

-\frac{2\rho n_{i}}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}\log\frac{1}{\rho}\geqslant-\frac{4\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}}{\gamma_{i}\varepsilon}\log\frac{1}{\rho_{\gamma}}\geqslant-\frac{4\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}}{\gamma_{i}\varepsilon}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon^{2}d}{k}=-4\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d\cdot\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}k}\geqslant-4\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d\kern 5.0pt.

For the
-3t
term, using
t=\varepsilon^{2}d/k
and
Lemma

C.2
we obtain

-\frac{2\rho n_{i}\cdot 3t}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}\geqslant-\frac{12\rho n_{i}\,t}{\gamma_{i}\,\varepsilon}=-\frac{12\rho n_{i}}{\gamma_{i}\,\varepsilon}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon^{2}d}{k}=-12\rho n_{i}\varepsilon d\cdot\beta_{i}\geqslant-6\,\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\,\varepsilon d\kern 5.0pt.

Finally, by
Lemma

C.1
, we have that

-2\rho n_{i}\sqrt{a+b}\geqslant-2\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\sqrt{3d}\geqslant-2\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d\kern 5.0pt.

Combining these three bounds in (
C.5
) yields

\langle Gy\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant-\Bigl(4+6+2\Bigr)\,\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d=-12\,\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d\geqslant-12\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d,

which is the desired inequality.
The probability statement is exactly that of
Theorem

C.8
with
t=\varepsilon^{2}d/k
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma C.10
.

Fix a level
i
and set
\beta_{i}=2^{-i}
and
n_{i}=2\beta_{i}n
.
Let
y\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
be a valid bisection labeling at level
i
.
Choose
0<\rho_{\gamma}\leqslant 10^{-6}
and set
t=\varepsilon^{2}d/k
.
Then, with probability at least

1-2e^{-t}-n_{i}^{-2}
,
the inequality

\langle Gy\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant-12\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d

holds simultaneously for every vector
z\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
with
\|z\|_{1}\ \leqslant\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}
.

Proof.

We discretize over the admissible
\ell_{1}
sizes:
since
z\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
,
the quantity
\|z\|_{1}
is an integer,
hence
\rho=\|z\|_{1}/n_{i}
ranges over the set
\{j/n_{i}:1\leqslant j\leqslant\lfloor\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\rfloor\}
.
For a fixed value
\rho
we invoke the level-
i
voting lower bound (Theorem
C.9
) with this
\rho
.
It states that, with probability at least
1-e^{-t\rho n_{i}}-n_{i}^{-3}
, the estimate

\langle Gy\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant-12\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\varepsilon d

holds simultaneously for all
z
with
\|z\|_{1}=\rho n_{i}
.
Taking a union bound over the at most
\lfloor\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\rfloor
distinct values,
the failure probability is at most

\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\rfloor}\Big(e^{-tj}+n_{i}^{-3}\Big)\leqslant\frac{e^{-t}}{1-e^{-t}}+\frac{\lfloor\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\rfloor}{n_{i}^{3}}\leqslant\frac{e^{-t}}{1-e^{-t}}+\frac{1}{n_{i}^{2}}\leqslant 2e^{-t}+\frac{1}{n_{i}^{2}}.

On the complementary event, the desired bound holds for every value in the set, and
hence for every
z\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
with
\|z\|_{1}\leqslant\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}
, as claimed.
∎

Next we give a lemma for lower bounding the inner product when the size of the set is large.

Lemma C.11
.

Fix a level
i
and set
\beta_{i}=2^{-i}
,
n_{i}=2\beta_{i}n
, and
k_{i}=\beta_{i}k
.
Let
y\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
be a valid bisection labeling at level
i
.
Let
\gamma\in\bigl[0,\,1-\frac{1000\,\chi\,k_{i}}{\varepsilon\sqrt{d_{i}}}\bigr]
.
Define

\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\gamma\,\beta_{i}\,\widetilde{C}_{i}}{2}\Bigr),\qquad t\coloneqq 0.001\,(1-\gamma)\,\beta_{i}\,\widetilde{C}_{i}.

Then for every
\rho\geqslant\rho_{\gamma}
, with probability at least

1-\exp(-t\rho n_{i})-\frac{1}{n_{i}^{3}}
, the inequality

\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,\,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{\varepsilon d}{5k}\,(1-\gamma)\,\rho_{\gamma}\,n_{i}

holds for all
z\in\{0,1\}^{n}
with
\|z\|_{1}=\rho n_{i}
, where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

Proof.

Let us fix
\rho\in[\rho_{\gamma},1]
and
z\in\{0,1\}^{n}
with
\|z\|_{1}=\rho n_{i}
.
By Theorem
C.8
applied to
\bar{G}
, with probability at least

1-e^{-t\rho n_{i}}-n_{i}^{-3}
we have

\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,\,z\rangle\geqslant 2\rho n_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}-\sqrt{a+b}}}\right),\qquad\widetilde{Q}_{i}\coloneqq\beta_{i}\widetilde{C}_{i}-\log\frac{1}{\rho}-3t.

(C.6)

Since
\rho\geqslant\rho_{\gamma}=\exp(-\gamma\,\beta_{i}\widetilde{C}_{i}/2)
, we have

-\log(1/\rho)\geqslant-\gamma\,\beta_{i}\widetilde{C}_{i}/2,

and hence

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{i}

\displaystyle\geqslant\Bigl(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}-0.003(1-\gamma)\Bigr)\,\beta_{i}\widetilde{C}_{i}

\displaystyle\geqslant 0.997\bigl(1-\gamma\bigr)\,\beta_{i}\widetilde{C}_{i}.

(C.7)

By Lemma
C.2
,
\log\widetilde{R}_{i}\leqslant\gamma_{i}\,\varepsilon\log 3+o(1)
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{Q}_{i}}{\log\widetilde{R}_{i}}

\displaystyle\geqslant 0.997\frac{(1-\gamma)\,\beta_{i}\,\widetilde{C}_{i}}{\gamma_{i}\,\varepsilon\log 3+o(1)}

\displaystyle\geqslant 0.997\cdot\frac{(\log 2)^{2}}{4\log 3}\,(1-\gamma)\,\frac{\varepsilon d}{k},

(C.8)

using

\beta_{i}\,\widetilde{C}_{i}\geqslant\frac{(\log 2)^{2}}{4}\,\frac{d\,\varepsilon^{2}}{\beta_{i}\,k^{2}}

by the monotonicity shown in
Theorem

C.5
.

For the last term, Lemma
C.1
gives
\sqrt{a+b\,}\leqslant\sqrt{3d}
.
We thus have

\sqrt{a+b}\leqslant\sqrt{3d}\leqslant 0.001\,(1-\gamma)\,\frac{\varepsilon d}{k}.

(C.9)

Combining (
C.6
), (
C.8
), and (
C.9
), we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,\,z\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant 2\rho n_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(0.109\,(1-\gamma)\,\frac{\varepsilon d}{k}-0.001\,(1-\gamma)\,\frac{\varepsilon d}{k}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{\varepsilon d}{5k}\,(1-\gamma)\,\rho n_{i}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{\varepsilon d}{5k}\,(1-\gamma)\,\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\kern 5.0pt,

as claimed.
∎

Corollary C.12
.

Fix a level
i
and set
\beta_{i}=2^{-i}
and
n_{i}=2\beta_{i}n
.
Let
y\in\{0,\pm 1\}^{n}
be a valid bisection labeling at level
i
.
Let
\gamma\in\bigl[0,\,1-\frac{1000\,\chi\,k_{i}}{\varepsilon\sqrt{d_{i}}}\bigr]
, and define

\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\exp\Bigl(-\tfrac{\gamma\,\beta_{i}\,\widetilde{C}_{i}}{2}\Bigr),\qquad t\coloneqq 0.001\,(1-\gamma)\,\beta_{i}\,\widetilde{C}_{i}.

Then, with probability at least
1-2e^{-t\,\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}}-n_{i}^{-2}
,
the inequality

\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{\varepsilon d}{5k}\,(1-\gamma)\,\rho_{\gamma}\,n_{i}

holds simultaneously for every vector
z\in\{0,1\}^{n}
with
\|z\|_{1}\geqslant\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}
, where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

Proof.

We discretize over the admissible
\ell_{1}
sizes.
Since
z\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, the quantity
\|z\|_{1}
is an integer, so

\rho=\|z\|_{1}/n_{i}
ranges over
\{j/n_{i}:\,m_{0}\leqslant j\leqslant n_{i}\}
,
where
m_{0}\coloneqq\lceil\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}\rceil
.
Fix
j\in\{m_{0},\ldots,n\}
and set
\rho=j/n_{i}\geqslant\rho_{\gamma}
.
By Lemma
C.11
(applied with our choice of
\rho
),
with probability at least

1-e^{-t\rho n_{i}}-n_{i}^{-3}=1-e^{-tj}-n_{i}^{-3}
, we have

\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{\varepsilon d}{5k}\,(1-\gamma)\,\rho_{\gamma}\,n_{i}\quad\text{for all }z\in\{0,1\}^{n}\text{ with }\|z\|_{1}=j.

Taking a union bound over all
j\in\{m_{0},\ldots,n_{i}\}
, the total failure probability is at most

\sum_{j=m_{0}}^{n_{i}}\bigl(e^{-tj}+n_{i}^{-3}\bigr)\leqslant\frac{e^{-tm_{0}}}{1-e^{-t}}+\frac{n_{i}-m_{0}+1}{n_{i}^{3}}\leqslant 2e^{-t\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}}+\frac{1}{n_{i}^{2}}.

On the complementary event, the claimed lower bound holds simultaneously for all sizes

j\geqslant m_{0}
, and hence for every
z\in\{0,1\}^{n}
with
\|z\|_{1}\geqslant\rho_{\gamma}n_{i}
, as desired.
∎

Now we conclude the proof of the majority voting lower bound of bisections.

Theorem

(Restatement of
Theorem

4.3
)
.

Fix a level
i
and set
\beta_{i}=2^{-i}
,
n_{i}=2\beta_{i}n
, and
k_{i}=\beta_{i}k
.
Let
\gamma\in[0,0.99]
.
Choose
\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\exp(-\gamma\beta_{i}\tilde{C_{i}}/2)
, and set
t=0.001(1-\gamma)\tilde{C_{i}}
.
Then with probability at least
1-\exp(-100k)-\frac{1}{n^{3}}
, for every
z\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
, and for every valid bisections
y\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,\pm 1}}\right\}^{n}
at level
i
. we have

\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{8k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{96\rho_{\gamma}kn_{i}}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,,

where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

Proof.

We first prove the claim for fixed bisection
y
.
When the size of the set is at least
\rho_{\gamma}n
, the lower bound easily follows from
Corollary

C.12
.
When the size of the set is at most
\rho_{\gamma}n
, the lower bound follows from
Lemma

C.10
with probability
1-\exp(-\varepsilon^{2}d/k)
, as
\lVert z\rVert_{1}\geqslant 0
.
From the proof of
Theorem

C.5
,
we know that
\beta_{i}\tilde{C_{i}}\geqslant\Omega(\varepsilon^{2}d_{i}/k_{i}^{2})\geqslant\Omega(2^{i}\log(k))
.
It follows that
\tilde{C_{i}}\rho_{\gamma}n\geqslant 100k\log(k)
.
Since there are at most
2^{2k}
possible choices for community bisections
y
,
we can take union bound and the claim follows.
∎

Finally we give the bound for larger values of
\gamma
which will be helpful for obtaining the optimal recovery rate in the final
k
-clustering.

Theorem

(Restatement of
Theorem

4.5
)
.

Let
C_{d,\varepsilon}=(\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b})^{2}
.
Let
\gamma\in[0,1-\frac{1000\chi k}{\varepsilon\sqrt{d}}]
.
Choose
\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\exp(-\gamma C_{d,\varepsilon}/k)
.
Then with probability at least
1-\exp(-100k)-\frac{1}{n^{3}}
, for every
z\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
, and for every pair of communities
y\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,\pm 1}}\right\}^{n}
.
We have

\langle\bar{G}y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{8k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{96\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,,

where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to
Theorem

4.3
.
However, now for the probability bound, we only need to use
\rho_{\gamma}n\geqslant 1
and
C\geqslant 100k
, and we only need to take union bound over
k^{2}
pairs of communities.
∎

Corollary

(Restatement of
Corollary

4.4
)
.

Fix a level
i
and set
\beta_{i}=2^{-i}
,
n_{i}=2\beta_{i}n
, and
k_{i}=\beta_{i}k
.
Let
\gamma\in[0,0.99]
.
Choose
\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\exp(-\gamma\beta_{i}\tilde{C_{i}}/2)
, and set
t=0.001(1-\gamma)\tilde{C_{i}}
.
Then with probability at least
1-\exp(-100k)-\frac{2}{n^{3}}
, for every
z\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
, for every
s\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
such that
\lVert s\rVert_{1}\geqslant(1-\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon}))n
, and for every valid bisections
y\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,\pm 1}}\right\}^{n}
at level
i
, we have

\langle\bar{G}\odot(ss^{\top})y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{16k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{640\rho_{\gamma}kn_{i}}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,,

where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

Proof.

Notice that

\displaystyle\langle\bar{G}\odot(ss^{\top})y\odot y,z\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\bar{G}\odot(yy^{\top}),(z\odot s)s^{\top}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\bar{G}\odot(yy^{\top}),(z\odot s)\mathbf{1}^{\top}\rangle-\langle\bar{G}\odot(yy^{\top}),(z\odot s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}\rangle\,.

By
Theorem

4.3
, it follows that, with probability
1-\exp(-100k)-\frac{1}{n^{3}}
,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{G}\odot(yy^{\top}),(z\odot s)\mathbf{1}^{\top}\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{8k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\odot s\rVert_{1}-\frac{96\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{8k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\lVert\mathbf{1}-s\rVert_{1}-\frac{96\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{8k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon})n-\frac{96\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{8k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{200\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,.

By
Theorem

C.7
, it follows that, with probability
1-\frac{1}{n^{3}}

\langle\bar{G}\odot(yy^{\top}),(z\odot s)(\mathbf{1}-s)^{\top}\rangle\leqslant\sqrt{2d}(\lVert z\rVert_{1}+\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon})n)\leqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{16k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}+\frac{100\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,.

Therefore, with probability
1-\exp(-100k)-\frac{2}{n^{3}}
,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{G}\odot(ss^{\top})y\odot y,z\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{8k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{200\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)-\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{16k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}+\frac{100\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{16k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{640\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,.

∎

Corollary

(Restatement of
Corollary

4.6
)
.

Let
\gamma\in[0,1-\frac{1000\chi k}{\varepsilon\sqrt{d}}]
.
Choose
\rho_{\gamma}\coloneqq\exp(-\gamma C)
.
Then with probability at least
1-\exp(-100k)-\frac{2}{n^{3}}
, for every
z\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
, for every
s\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,1}}\right\}^{n}
such that
\lVert s\rVert_{1}\geqslant(1-\exp(-2C_{d,\varepsilon}))n
, and for every pair of communities
y\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{0,\pm 1}}\right\}^{n}
.
We have

\langle\bar{G}\odot(ss^{\top})y\odot y,z\rangle\geqslant\frac{(1-\gamma)\varepsilon d}{16k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lVert z\rVert_{1}-\frac{640\rho_{\gamma}kn}{1-\gamma}}}\right)\,,

where
\bar{G}
is the centered adjacency matrix.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to
Corollary

4.4
. The only difference is that the voting lower bound is via
Theorem

4.5
.
∎
